Since many years it has been used in army and so it is also known as Army organization. In this kind of organization, the centralization of power is done on top level. The distribution of power and responsibilities is done in straight line from top to bottom level. As the distribution of power is in straight line from top to bottom level, it is called Linear organization. In this, each employee is answerable to his or her superior authority. In this type, higher degree of power can be seen on top level and lower amount of power is seen on the bottom level.\r\n## Formation :\r\nIn this type of organization, entire business unit is classified in different department. As per each section or area, a separate departmental head is appointed. This departmental head is answerable to the superior head of his or her own area. The area head is given all the necessary power needed as per his or her department. In this type of organization, division is not done according to the work but according to departments. Hence it is also known as departmental organization. The Linear organization structure can be best seen with the help of the following diagram :\r\n\r\nIn Linear organization, the greatest power is seen with the Board of Directors. They take policy based decisions. The General Manager is assigned necessary powers from them. The General Manager is the main Administrative Head, whose work is to ensure that the lower employees follow the task and decisions taken by the board of director. The above given chart is divided into production department, administrative department and sales department, three parts. As per the important work in each department, Purchase, Production or Sales officers etc. are appointed. Such officers carry the entire responsibility of their own sections. Under such officers, supervisors, foreman and clerks, etc. work. Under the foreman, workers work. In this way, Linear organization ensures power from top to bottom and responsibilities flow on from bottom to top level.\r\n\r\nSuch kind of structure is more favourable where there is a small size of the organization and the work area is also limited and the problems of control and discipline are also limited.\n\nAdditional Instructions:\n","timestamp":"2025-05-07 14:29:31"},{"sender":"user","message":"","timestamp":"2025-05-07 14:29:43"},{"sender":"ai","message":"# Linear Organization\n\n## Meaning :\n\nLinear organization, also known as ***Army organization***, is the ***most simple*** and ***ancient*** type of organizational structure. It has been traditionally used in the army for many years.\n\nIn this structure, the ***centralization of power*** rests at the ***top level***. The ***distribution of power*** and ***responsibilities*** flows in a ***straight line*** from the ***top level*** down to the ***bottom level***. This straight-line flow is why it's called Linear organization.\n\nA key characteristic is that each employee is ***answerable*** directly to their immediate ***superior authority***. You will observe a ***higher degree of power*** at the top levels and a ***lower amount of power*** at the bottom levels.\n\n---\n\n**MEMORY TIP:** Think of an *Army Chain of Command*. Orders (Power\/Responsibility) flow directly *down* from the General (Top) to the soldiers (Bottom), and reports (Answerability) flow *up* the chain.\n\n---\n\n## Formation :\n\nThe formation of Linear organization involves structuring the entire business unit by classifying it into different ***departments***.\n\n*   For each specific ***section*** or ***area***, a separate ***departmental head*** is appointed.\n*   This ***departmental head*** is directly ***answerable*** to the ***superior head*** of their respective area.\n*   The ***area head*** is granted all the necessary ***power*** required for their specific department.\n\nAn important point regarding formation is that ***division is not done according to the work*** or function, but ***according to departments***. This is why Linear organization is also sometimes referred to as ***departmental organization***.\n\nThe structure demonstrates a clear hierarchy:\n\n*   The ***Board of Directors*** holds the ***greatest power*** and makes ***policy-based decisions***.\n*   The ***General Manager*** receives necessary ***powers*** from the Board. The General Manager acts as the main ***Administrative Head*** responsible for ensuring that lower-level employees implement the decisions and tasks set by the Board.\n*   Below the General Manager, the organization is often divided into major departments like Production, Administrative, and Sales (as described in the typical chart).\n*   Within each department, officers such as ***Purchase, Production, or Sales officers*** are appointed based on the important work required.\n*   These officers hold the ***entire responsibility*** for their specific sections.\n*   Working under these officers are ***supervisors, foremen, and clerks***.\n*   Finally, the ***workers*** are at the lowest level, reporting to the foreman.\n\nIn essence, in Linear organization, ***power flows from top to bottom***, while ***responsibilities and answerability flow from bottom to top level***.\n\nThis type of structure is most suitable for organizations that are of a ***small size***, have a ***limited work area***, and face ***limited problems of control and discipline***.","timestamp":"2025-05-07 14:29:43"}]

As the distribution of power is in straight line from top to bottom level, it is called Linear organization. In this, each employee is answerable to his or her superior authority. In this type, higher degree of power can be seen on top level and lower amount of power is seen on the bottom level.\r\n## Formation :\r\nIn this type of organization, entire business unit is classified in different department. It has been used for many years, particularly in the **army**, which is why it is also known as **Army Organization**.\n\nHere are the key characteristics of Linear Organization:\n\n*   **Centralization of Power:** Power is concentrated at the *top level* of the organization.\n*   **Distribution of Power and Responsibilities:** Power and responsibilities flow in a *straight line* from the *top level* down to the *bottom level*.\n*   **Accountability:** Each employee is directly answerable to their immediate **superior authority**.\n*   **Power Hierarchy:** A **higher degree of power** is held at the *top level*, and the amount of power gradually decreases towards the *bottom level*.\n\n---\n\n### MEMORY TIP (Meaning)\n\nRemember \"LINE\": **L**ike **I**n the **N**avy\/Army, **E**asy structure (Simple, Top-Down Authority).\n\n---\n\n## Formation\n\nIn the *Formation* of a Linear Organization, the entire business unit is structured based on departments.\n\nKey aspects of its formation are:\n\n*   **Departmental Classification:** The business unit is divided into different **departments** or sections (e.g., Production, Sales, Administration).\n*   **Appointment of Departmental Heads:** A separate **departmental head** is appointed for each section or area.\n*   **Accountability of Heads:** Each departmental head is answerable to their **superior head** responsible for that area.\n*   **Delegation of Power:** The area head is given all the *necessary power* required to manage their specific department.\n*   **Basis of Division:** The organization is divided according to **departments**, *not* specifically according to the type of work performed across different departments. Hence, it is also known as **Departmental Organization**.\n\nThe structure typically follows a hierarchical flow from top to bottom:\n\n1.  **Board of Directors:** Hold the *greatest power* and make **policy-based decisions**.\n2.  **General Manager:** Assigned necessary powers by the Board. Acts as the **main Administrative Head**, ensuring decisions are followed by lower levels.\n3.  **Departmental Heads:** (e.g., Production Head, Administrative Head, Sales Head) Report to the General Manager and manage their respective departments.\n4.  **Officers:** (e.g., Purchase Officer, Production Officer, Sales Officer) Appointed within departments based on important work. They carry the *entire responsibility* for their sections.\n5.  **Supervisors, Foreman, Clerks:** Work under the officers.\n6.  **Workers:** Work under the foreman.\n\nIn this structure:\n\n*   **Power** flows **from top to bottom**.\n*   **Responsibilities** flow **from bottom to top** (as each level is accountable to its superior).\n\n---\n\n### Suitable Situations\n\nThis structure is more favorable in organizations that have a:\n\n*   *Small size*.\n*   *Limited work area*.\n*   *Limited problems* related to control and discipline.\n\n---\n\n### MEMORY TIP (Formation)\n\nThink of a pyramid: **Top** (Board) -> **Middle** (GM, Dept Heads, Officers) -> **Bottom** (Workers). As the distribution of power is in straight line from top to bottom level, it is called Linear organization. In this, each employee is answerable to his or her superior authority. In this type, higher degree of power can be seen on top level and lower amount of power is seen on the bottom level.\r\n## Formation :\r\nIn this type of organization, entire business unit is classified in different department. Under such officers, supervisors, foreman and clerks, etc. work. Under the foreman, workers work. It has been used for many years, particularly in the **army**, which is why it is also known as **Army Organization**.\n\nHere are the key characteristics of Linear Organization:\n\n*   **Centralization of Power:** Power is concentrated at the *top level* of the organization.\n*   **Distribution of Power and Responsibilities:** Power and responsibilities flow in a *straight line* from the *top level* down to the *bottom level*.\n*   **Accountability:** Each employee is directly answerable to their immediate **superior authority**.\n*   **Power Hierarchy:** A **higher degree of power** is held at the *top level*, and the amount of power gradually decreases towards the *bottom level*.\n\n---\n\n### MEMORY TIP (Meaning)\n\nRemember \"LINE\": **L**ike **I**n the **N**avy\/Army, **E**asy structure (Simple, Top-Down Authority).\n\n---\n\n## Formation\n\nIn the *Formation* of a Linear Organization, the entire business unit is structured based on departments.\n\nKey aspects of its formation are:\n\n*   **Departmental Classification:** The business unit is divided into different **departments** or sections (e.g., Production, Sales, Administration).\n*   **Appointment of Departmental Heads:** A separate **departmental head** is appointed for each section or area.\n*   **Accountability of Heads:** Each departmental head is answerable to their **superior head** responsible for that area.\n*   **Delegation of Power:** The area head is given all the *necessary power* required to manage their specific department.\n*   **Basis of Division:** The organization is divided according to **departments**, *not* specifically according to the type of work performed across different departments. Hence, it is also known as **Departmental Organization**.\n\nThe structure typically follows a hierarchical flow from top to bottom:\n\n1.  **Board of Directors:** Hold the *greatest power* and make **policy-based decisions**.\n2.  **General Manager:** Assigned necessary powers by the Board. Acts as the **main Administrative Head**, ensuring decisions are followed by lower levels.\n3.  **Departmental Heads:** (e.g., Production Head, Administrative Head, Sales Head) Report to the General Manager and manage their respective departments.\n4.  **Officers:** (e.g., Purchase Officer, Production Officer, Sales Officer) Appointed within departments based on important work. They carry the *entire responsibility* for their sections.\n5.  **Supervisors, Foreman, Clerks:** Work under the officers.\n6.  **Workers:** Work under the foreman.\n\nIn this structure:\n\n*   **Power** flows **from top to bottom**.\n*   **Responsibilities** flow **from bottom to top** (as each level is accountable to its superior).\n\n---\n\n### Suitable Situations\n\nThis structure is more favorable in organizations that have a:\n\n*   *Small size*.\n*   *Limited work area*.\n*   *Limited problems* related to control and discipline.\n\n---\n\n### MEMORY TIP (Formation)\n\nThink of a pyramid: **Top** (Board) -> **Middle** (GM, Dept Heads, Officers) -> **Bottom** (Workers). It has been used for many years, particularly in the **army**, which is why it is also known as **Army Organization**.\n\nHere are the key characteristics of Linear Organization:\n\n*   **Centralization of Power:** Power is concentrated at the *top level* of the organization.\n*   **Distribution of Power and Responsibilities:** Power and responsibilities flow in a *straight line* from the *top level* down to the *bottom level*.\n*   **Accountability:** Each employee is directly answerable to their immediate **superior authority**.\n*   **Power Hierarchy:** A **higher degree of power** is held at the *top level*, and the amount of power gradually decreases towards the *bottom level*.\n\n---\n\n### MEMORY TIP (Meaning)\n\nRemember \"LINE\": **L**ike **I**n the **N**avy\/Army, **E**asy structure (Simple, Top-Down Authority).\n\n---\n\n## Formation\n\nIn the *Formation* of a Linear Organization, the entire business unit is structured based on departments.\n\nKey aspects of its formation are:\n\n*   **Departmental Classification:** The business unit is divided into different **departments** or sections (e.g., Production, Sales, Administration).\n*   **Appointment of Departmental Heads:** A separate **departmental head** is appointed for each section or area.\n*   **Accountability of Heads:** Each departmental head is answerable to their **superior head** responsible for that area.\n*   **Delegation of Power:** The area head is given all the *necessary power* required to manage their specific department.\n*   **Basis of Division:** The organization is divided according to **departments**, *not* specifically according to the type of work performed across different departments. Hence, it is also known as **Departmental Organization**.\n\nThe structure typically follows a hierarchical flow from top to bottom:\n\n1.  **Board of Directors:** Hold the *greatest power* and make **policy-based decisions**.\n2.  **General Manager:** Assigned necessary powers by the Board. Acts as the **main Administrative Head**, ensuring decisions are followed by lower levels.\n3.  **Departmental Heads:** (e.g., Production Head, Administrative Head, Sales Head) Report to the General Manager and manage their respective departments.\n4.  **Officers:** (e.g., Purchase Officer, Production Officer, Sales Officer) Appointed within departments based on important work. They carry the *entire responsibility* for their sections.\n5.  **Supervisors, Foreman, Clerks:** Work under the officers.\n6.  **Workers:** Work under the foreman.\n\nIn this structure:\n\n*   **Power** flows **from top to bottom**.\n*   **Responsibilities** flow **from bottom to top** (as each level is accountable to its superior).\n\n---\n\n### Suitable Situations\n\nThis structure is more favorable in organizations that have a:\n\n*   *Small size*.\n*   *Limited work area*.\n*   *Limited problems* related to control and discipline.\n\n---\n\n### MEMORY TIP (Formation)\n\nThink of a pyramid: **Top** (Board) -> **Middle** (GM, Dept Heads, Officers) -> **Bottom** (Workers). It has been used for many years, particularly in the **army**, which is why it is also known as **Army Organization**.\n\nHere are the key characteristics of Linear Organization:\n\n*   **Centralization of Power:** Power is concentrated at the *top level* of the organization.\n*   **Distribution of Power and Responsibilities:** Power and responsibilities flow in a *straight line* from the *top level* down to the *bottom level*.\n*   **Accountability:** Each employee is directly answerable to their immediate **superior authority**.\n*   **Power Hierarchy:** A **higher degree of power** is held at the *top level*, and the amount of power gradually decreases towards the *bottom level*.\n\n---\n\n### MEMORY TIP (Meaning)\n\nRemember \"LINE\": **L**ike **I**n the **N**avy\/Army, **E**asy structure (Simple, Top-Down Authority).\n\n---\n\n## Formation\n\nIn the *Formation* of a Linear Organization, the entire business unit is structured based on departments.\n\nKey aspects of its formation are:\n\n*   **Departmental Classification:** The business unit is divided into different **departments** or sections (e.g., Production, Sales, Administration).\n*   **Appointment of Departmental Heads:** A separate **departmental head** is appointed for each section or area.\n*   **Accountability of Heads:** Each departmental head is answerable to their **superior head** responsible for that area.\n*   **Delegation of Power:** The area head is given all the *necessary power* required to manage their specific department.\n*   **Basis of Division:** The organization is divided according to **departments**, *not* specifically according to the type of work performed across different departments. Hence, it is also known as **Departmental Organization**.\n\nThe structure typically follows a hierarchical flow from top to bottom:\n\n1.  **Board of Directors:** Hold the *greatest power* and make **policy-based decisions**.\n2.  **General Manager:** Assigned necessary powers by the Board. Acts as the **main Administrative Head**, ensuring decisions are followed by lower levels.\n3.  **Departmental Heads:** (e.g., Production Head, Administrative Head, Sales Head) Report to the General Manager and manage their respective departments.\n4.  **Officers:** (e.g., Purchase Officer, Production Officer, Sales Officer) Appointed within departments based on important work. They carry the *entire responsibility* for their sections.\n5.  **Supervisors, Foreman, Clerks:** Work under the officers.\n6.  **Workers:** Work under the foreman.\n\nIn this structure:\n\n*   **Power** flows **from top to bottom**.\n*   **Responsibilities** flow **from bottom to top** (as each level is accountable to its superior).\n\n---\n\n### Suitable Situations\n\nThis structure is more favorable in organizations that have a:\n\n*   *Small size*.\n*   *Limited work area*.\n*   *Limited problems* related to control and discipline.\n\n---\n\n### MEMORY TIP (Formation)\n\nThink of a pyramid: **Top** (Board) -> **Middle** (GM, Dept Heads, Officers) -> **Bottom** (Workers). Power goes down, responsibility goes up. Division is by *Department* (P-A-S), not task.","timestamp":"2025-05-07 14:34:20"},{"sender":"user","message":"where is code or path taht i told you to fethc \n\n\n8. Since many years it has been used in army and so it is also known as Army organization. In this kind of organization, the centralization of power is done on top level. The distribution of power and responsibilities is done in straight line from top to bottom level. As the distribution of power is in straight line from top to bottom level, it is called Linear organization. In this, each employee is answerable to his or her superior authority. In this type, higher degree of power can be seen on top level and lower amount of power is seen on the bottom level.\r\n## Formation :\r\nIn this type of organization, entire business unit is classified in different department. As per each section or area, a separate departmental head is appointed. This departmental head is answerable to the superior head of his or her own area. The area head is given all the necessary power needed as per his or her department. In this type of organization, division is not done according to the work but according to departments. Hence it is also known as departmental organization. The Linear organization structure can be best seen with the help of the following diagram :\r\n\r\nIn Linear organization, the greatest power is seen with the Board of Directors. They take policy based decisions. The General Manager is assigned necessary powers from them. The General Manager is the main Administrative Head, whose work is to ensure that the lower employees follow the task and decisions taken by the board of director. As the distribution of power is in straight line from top to bottom level, it is called Linear organization. In this, each employee is answerable to his or her superior authority. The distribution of power and responsibilities is done in straight line from top to bottom level. As the distribution of power is in straight line from top to bottom level, it is called Linear organization. In this, each employee is answerable to his or her superior authority. In this type, higher degree of power can be seen on top level and lower amount of power is seen on the bottom level.\r\n## Formation :\r\nIn this type of organization, entire business unit is classified in different department. As per each section or area, a separate departmental head is appointed. This departmental head is answerable to the superior head of his or her own area. The area head is given all the necessary power needed as per his or her department. In this type of organization, division is not done according to the work but according to departments. Hence it is also known as departmental organization. Under such officers, supervisors, foreman and clerks, etc. work. Under the foreman, workers work. In this way, Linear organization ensures power from top to bottom and responsibilities flow on from bottom to top level.\r\n\r\nSuch kind of structure is more favourable where there is a small size of the organization and the work area is also limited and the problems of control and discipline are also limited.\n\nAdditional Instructions:\n","timestamp":"2025-05-07 16:52:34"},{"sender":"user","message":"","timestamp":"2025-05-07 16:52:53"},{"sender":"ai","message":"Sorry, I encountered an error trying to process your request. In the beginning, the companies of developed countries used to set up industries or start selling their goods and services in developing and undeveloped countries for which developing and underdeveloped countries give permission. Subsequently, developing and uner developed countries also allow their companies to do business in other countries. Actually, globalization is a two way activity. When multi national companies get permission to start industries\r\n\r\nin other countries, in the same way domestic companies can also do so in developed countries. In this way internationalization of industries can be seen.\r\n\r\nTo develop business and industry of the entire world, an arrangement was needed at the international level which resulted in the formation of World Trade Organisation (WTO). This organization takes effort to develop business and trade all over the world. It enables its member countries to eliminate the barriers that arise in the path of globalization. Since many years it has been used in army and so it is also known as Army organization. In this kind of organization, the centralization of power is done on top level. The distribution of power and responsibilities is done in straight line from top to bottom level. As the distribution of power is in straight line from top to bottom level, it is called Linear organization. In this, each employee is answerable to his or her superior authority. In this type, higher degree of power can be seen on top level and lower amount of power is seen on the bottom level.\r\n## Formation :\r\nIn this type of organization, entire business unit is classified in different department. This straight flow is why it is called **Linear organization**.\n*   Each employee in this setup is ***answerable*** directly to their **superior authority**.\n*   A ***higher degree of power*** is observed at the ***top level***, while a ***lower amount of power*** is found at the ***bottom level***.\n\n***\n\n### MEMORY TIP (Meaning):\n\nThink of an **ARMY (Army Organization)** where orders flow in a **STRAIGHT LINE (Linear)** from the **GENERAL (Top)** down to the **SOLDIERS (Bottom)**. Everyone answers to their immediate boss (**Superior Authority**). Power is **CENTRALISED** at the top.\n\n***\n\n## Formation :\n\n*   In **Linear organization**, the *entire business unit* is first classified into **different departments**.\n*   For each *section or area*, a separate **departmental head** is appointed.\n*   This **departmental head** is ***answerable*** to the **superior head** of their specific area.\n*   The **area head** is granted *all the necessary power* required to manage their department effectively.\n*   A key characteristic is that the *division* in this structure is done **according to departments**, *not according to the work process*.\n*   Due to this departmental division, it is also known as **Departmental organization**.\n*   The structure flows hierarchically from the top:\n    *   At the very top are the **Board of Directors**, who hold the *greatest power* and make *policy-based decisions*.\n    *   The **General Manager** is assigned the necessary powers by the Board of Directors and serves as the *main Administrative Head*. Their role is to ensure that *lower employees follow the tasks and decisions* made by the board.\n    *   The organization is divided into main departments (e.g., **production department**, **administrative department**, and **sales department**).\n    *   According to the important work in each department, specific officers like **Purchase, Production or Sales officers** are appointed.\n    *   These officers bear the ***entire responsibility*** for their own sections.\n    *   Under these officers, different levels of employees work, such as **supervisors**, **foreman**, and **clerks**.\n    *   Under the **foreman**, the actual **workers** perform the tasks.\n*   In **Linear organization**, **power** flows in a **straight line from top to bottom**, while **responsibilities** flow **from bottom to top level**.\n*   This type of structure is more *favourable* for organizations with a ***small size***, a ***limited work area***, and where *problems of control and discipline* are also ***limited***.\n\n***\n\n### MEMORY TIP (Formation):\n\nRemember the flow like a *pyramid*: **BOD** (Board) $\\rightarrow$ **GM** (General Manager) $\\rightarrow$ **Dept. This straight-line flow gives it the name **Linear organization**.\n*   **Accountability:** Each employee is directly **answerable to his or her superior authority** (their immediate boss) in the chain of command.\n*   **Power Distribution:** There is a higher degree of **power** concentrated at the **top level** and a progressively lower amount of **power** as one moves down to the **bottom level**.\n\n---\n\n### MEMORY TIP:\n*Linear* means straight line. Think of an *Army* chain of command: orders go down (power), reports go up (responsibility), everyone answers to their immediate *superior*.\n\n---\n\n## Formation :\n\nThe formation of Linear organization involves structuring the business unit based on distinct departments and establishing a clear, direct chain of command within them.\n\n*   **Departmental Classification:** The entire business unit is divided into different **departments** or sections (e.g., Production, Sales, Administration).\n*   **Departmental Heads:** A separate **departmental head** is appointed for each section or area.\n*   **Accountability of Heads:** This **departmental head** is directly **answerable** to the **superior head** who oversees their specific area or function.\n*   **Delegation of Power:** The **area head** or departmental head is given all the **necessary power** required to manage their respective department effectively.\n*   **Basis of Division:** This structure is organized according to **departments**, rather than strictly by the type of work being performed. Due to this departmental basis, it is also known as **departmental organization**.\n*   **Hierarchical Structure (Example):**\n    *   The **Board of Directors** holds the greatest power and makes **policy-based decisions**.\n    *   The **General Manager** is delegated necessary powers by the Board of Directors and acts as the main **Administrative Head**. Their role is to ensure that the decisions and tasks set by the Board of Directors are followed by the lower employees.\n    *   Under the General Manager, heads of major departments (like **Production department**, **Administrative department**, **Sales department**) are appointed as **officers** (e.g., Purchase Officer, Production Officer, Sales Officer). These officers have the responsibility for their entire sections.\n    *   Working under these officers are **supervisors**, **foremen**, and **clerks**, etc.\n    *   Under the foreman, the **workers** perform the actual tasks.\n*   **Flow of Power and Responsibility:** In this structure, **power** flows downwards from the **top to the bottom** level, while **responsibilities** flow upwards from the **bottom to the top** level.\n*   **Suitability:** This type of structure is generally more favorable for organizations that are **small in size**, have a **limited work area**, and where problems related to **control** and **discipline** are also limited.\n\n---\n\n### MEMORY TIP:\nFormation is *Department*-based. It has been traditionally used in the *army* for many years, which is why it is also commonly known as **Army organization**.\n\n*   In this structure, the **centralization of power** is significantly high at the *top level*.\n*   The **distribution of power** and **responsibilities** follows a *straight line* path, flowing directly from the *top level* down to the *bottom level*. This direct flow is the reason it's called **Linear organization**.\n*   Each employee in the hierarchy is **answerable** directly to their immediate **superior authority**.\n*   Consequently, a *higher degree of power* is concentrated at the *top level*, while a *lower amount of power* is present at the *bottom level*.\n\n**MEMORY TIP:** Think of a *straight line* or a *chain of command* like in the *army*. Power flows *down* the line, accountability flows *up* the line.\n\n---\n\n## Formation\n\nIn the **Formation** of a **Linear organization**, the entire business unit is typically divided and classified into different **departments**.\n\n*   For each distinct section or area (department), a separate **departmental head** is appointed.\n*   This **departmental head** is directly **answerable** to the **superior head** responsible for their specific area or department.\n*   The area or departmental head is granted all the necessary **power** required to manage their department effectively.\n*   A key characteristic of this structure is that the division within the organization is based on **departments** rather than primarily on the type of **work**. Due to this departmental basis, it is also referred to as **departmental organization**.\n\nThe structure of **Linear organization** can be clearly visualized with the help of a diagram:\n\n```image\n\/main\/public\/images\/12gsebeng\/BA\/Organising\/Linear_Organisation\/linear_organisation.png\n```\n\n*   At the apex of the structure is the **Board of Directors**, holding the **greatest power** and making **policy-based decisions**.\n*   Reporting directly to the Board is the **General Manager**, who is assigned the necessary powers by the Board. The **General Manager** acts as the *main Administrative Head*, responsible for ensuring that the tasks and decisions made by the **Board of Directors** are implemented and followed by employees at lower levels.\n*   The chart typically shows divisions like the **Production Department**, **Administrative Department**, and **Sales Department**, all reporting to the General Manager.\n*   Within each department, officers like **Purchase Officer**, **Production Officer**, **Sales Officer**, or **Advertisement Officer** are appointed based on the important work areas. These officers bear the entire **responsibility** for their specific sections.\n*   Working under these officers are positions such as **supervisors**, **foreman**, and **clerks**.\n*   At the lowest level, **workers** typically work under the **foreman**.\n*   This structure clearly illustrates the flow of **power from top to bottom** and the flow of **responsibilities from bottom to top level**.\n\nThis type of organizational structure is *more favourable* and effective in situations where:\n\n*   The **size of the organization** is **small**.\n*   The **work area** or scope of operations is **limited**.\n*   The problems related to **control and discipline** are also **limited**.\n\n**MEMORY TIP:**\n*   **Formation:** Remember it's divided by *Departments* (like school *departments* - Science, Arts), leading to the name \"**Departmental Organization**\". Each head reports to *their* superior.\n*   **Flow:** Power flows *down* (BOD -> GM -> Officers -> Workers), Responsibility flows *up* (Workers -> Foreman -> ... -> BOD). Think \"Power down, Responsibility up.\"\n*   **Suitability:** Simple structure suits *small size*, *limited area*, *easy control*.
